Phase 3.1A is **committed and pushed** as [`eede2009`](https://github.com/Sohaib7932/trace/commit/eede2009e46b3ad249b1aea8991687a713f206ef) on `feat/phase-3-1-live-ai-integration`. The commit includes the 24 deliverables and genuine shared log. No PR was opened or merged.

The Phase 3.1B audit is complete. **Internal provider integration can proceed after approval; live learner activation needs additional compatibility work.** No audit implementation changes were made.

**1. Exact integration points**

| Area | Existing source |
|---|---|
| Shared client and transport | [client.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/ai/client.py:38) |
| Transport request, result and usage schemas | [ai_client.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/schemas/ai_client.py:29) |
| AI configuration | [ai_settings.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/core/ai_settings.py:14) |
| Generation adapter | [bounded_question_generator.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/ai/providers/bounded_question_generator.py:32) |
| Tutor and free-form lesson adapters | [bounded_tutor.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/ai/providers/bounded_tutor.py:84) |
| Public lesson-context builder and service | [lesson_assistant.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/services/lesson_assistant.py:22) |
| Support service and retrieval | [support.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/services/support.py:15), [support_knowledge.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/repositories/support_knowledge.py:44) |
| Application lifecycle and dependencies | [main.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/main.py:20), [dependencies.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/api/dependencies.py:52) |

**2. Current client architecture**

The shared boundary is:

```text
BoundedAIClient.complete(capability, instructions, data, output_schema, subject)
    → AITransport.complete(TransportRequest)
    → TransportCompletion
    → ClientCompletion
```

It already handles request/output limits, model routing, sanitized errors, bounded retries, deadlines, rate counters and usage accounting.

Live operation is deliberately blocked:

- Settings allow only `disabled` and `mock`.
- `AITransport.source` accepts only `test_fixture`.
- The client rejects non-fixture transports.
- Returned provider/source metadata is hardcoded to mock/fixture values.

A provider SDK adapter alone therefore cannot enable live operation.

**3. Required provider-specific implementation**

After approval, implement one transport and a small, reviewed extension to those activation gates. It must:

- Load the approved credential server-side without exposing its value.
- Use the exact approved models.
- Preserve original generation response text for admission.
- Map refusals, truncation and failures into existing typed results.
- Supply genuine provider/model/request/usage metadata.
- Enforce actual connection/read deadlines and bounded response reads.
- Prevent SDK retries from multiplying wrapper retries.
- Define pricing, cost reservations and budget scope.

The current timeout check rejects late results after transport returns; it cannot interrupt an SDK that ignores its timeout contract. Returned model IDs must also satisfy the client’s exact-match check—alias resolution needs an explicit policy.

**4. Dependency injection and application wiring**

Application startup currently manages database resources only. No shared AI client, generation factory, LessonAssistant or SupportAssistant is registered. The tutor dependency always returns `FakeTutorProvider`.

The shared client should be reused through application lifecycle ownership. Constructing it per request would reset its rate and spending counters. Those counters are currently process-local.

Generation uses the `"internal"` subject; tutoring also defaults to `"internal"`. Public integrations need server-owned user/learner subjects and capability-specific limits.

**5. Generation readiness**

Preserve the existing sequence:

```text
GenerationRequest → QuestionGenerator → ChallengeContentAdmission
→ compose_lesson_spec → CandidateValidationService
```

The adapter already preserves raw output for duplicate-key, size, schema and reference checks. No replacement parser or second generation protocol is needed.

Actual challenge generation still requires:

- Approved complete Notes and Tasks production blueprints.
- Trusted brief construction and server-reserved identities.
- Exact transfer-target identity/version/digest.
- Internal orchestration with `IsolatedCandidateRuntime` injected.

Complete blueprints currently exist only as test fixtures. They must remain test-only. Persistence, publication and assignment remain deferred, with `release_eligible=False`.

**6. Tutoring and existing hint-route risks**

The three existing `TutorProvider` signatures can remain intact. Free-form questions already have a separate `LessonAssistant` with bounded public context.

Public hint activation needs more than changing dependency injection:

- [LearningService.hint](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/services/learning.py:177) rejects non-fixture responses.
- `HintResponse.provider` accepts only `"fake"`.
- Saved interactions default to fake provider/fixture provenance.
- New tutoring adapters retain `source`, but discard transport model/request metadata.
- Frontend types and guidance wording are fixture-only.
- Lesson Specification V1 still requires `TutorPolicy.mode="authored_only"`.

**The provider call currently runs inside the learner write transaction.** [SqlLearningRepository.write](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/db/learning.py:290) acquires SQLite’s write gate before that call. Network latency and retries would hold it.

Before public activation, review transaction reservation/finalization, concurrency and provenance. Preserve ownership, prediction-first behavior, exact idempotent retries, sequential hints, terminal-session restrictions and the three-request guidance budget. Failed requests currently consume that budget.

**7. Support readiness**

Support is separate and stateless. Its model selects retrieved passage IDs; the server returns exact quotations and source references. Preserve this grounding contract.

Production source inventory and escalation configuration are empty. Required approvals include:

- Product overview and learner navigation.
- Login/Google troubleshooting.
- Progress, hints and learning-cycle explanations.
- Common technical problems.
- Accurate daily-exercise availability.
- A human support destination.

The escalation contract currently accepts a label and public HTTPS URL. Internal architecture/operator documents are not approved support sources.

Any future support endpoint should use required account authentication, existing write-origin protections, account action limits and a server-owned account subject. It should avoid learner-creation dependencies and expose no document-selection parameters.

**8. Configuration and dependencies**

Existing variables use the `TRACE_LLM_` prefix:

```text
MODE
PROVIDER_ID
GENERATION_MODEL_ID
CONVERSATIONAL_MODEL_ID
CREDENTIAL_ENV_VAR
MAX_REQUEST_BYTES / MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES
MAX_INPUT_TOKENS / MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS
CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS / RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS / TOTAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS
MAX_RETRIES
REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE / MAX_SUBJECTS
SPEND_LIMIT_MICROUSD / MAX_COST_PER_ATTEMPT_MICROUSD
```

The credential field contains a variable **name**, not its value. [ai.env.example](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/ai.env.example) is inactive; its keys cannot simply be copied into the existing strict application `.env` schema.

Verified installed/locked versions include Python 3.13.5, FastAPI 0.162.0, Pydantic 2.14.2, pydantic-settings 2.16.2, SQLAlchemy 2.0.55 and `httpx2` 2.16.0.

No vendor SDK or conventional `httpx` is installed. After provider approval, check its official SDK requirements against Python 3.13/Pydantic 2. Existing `httpx2` must not be assumed interchangeable with an SDK’s conventional `httpx` dependency.

**9. Team decisions still required**

Provider name, exact generation and conversational model IDs, credential environment-variable name, spending cap and its window/scope. Production blueprints, support documents and escalation destination also remain outstanding.

**10. Minimal plan for tomorrow**

1. Confirm those provider and budget decisions.
2. Review the selected provider’s official API/SDK requirements.
3. Extend live-mode configuration and truthful provenance.
4. Implement and mock-test one bounded transport.
5. Add application-scoped lifecycle/DI and subject binding.
6. Validate internal generation, tutoring and support operations separately.
7. Review hint-route transaction/API/provenance compatibility before activation.
8. Run regressions, static checks and OpenAPI comparison; perform a bounded real smoke test only with explicit credential/spending authorization.

No tests were rerun for this read-only audit. The prior **1,601 passing tests, 258 new tests, Ruff and mypy results** remain supported by the verified unchanged source snapshot. All **310 audited files** retained identical content, size and modification times. Four pre-existing untracked log artifacts remain preserved.

Stopped after the audit.

# TRACE — PROVIDER-INDEPENDENT BACKEND COMPLETION

## Mission

Complete the remaining backend engineering work that can be safely implemented WITHOUT a live LLM provider, provider credentials, paid API usage, or frontend changes.

The objective is to make Trace's existing AI foundation usable by future provider integrations while completing the trusted lesson, generation, tutoring, support and assignment infrastructure.

This is a multi-milestone engineering task. Work incrementally, validate every milestone and preserve backward compatibility.

Do not attempt a giant rewrite.

---

# PART 0 — CRITICAL REPOSITORY SAFETY

Before making changes:

1. Inspect the Git branch, remote configuration, working tree and recent commits.
2. Identify the Phase 3.1A commit:
   `eede2009e46b3ad249b1aea8991687a713f206ef`
3. Identify existing Phase 3.1A changes and keep them separate from this work.
4. Determine whether the Phase 3.1A PR has been created and whether its branch is available remotely.
5. Preserve all uncommitted modifications, untracked files, shared Codex logs, databases, migrations, environment files and user-created work.
6. Never use:
   - `git reset --hard`
   - `git clean`
   - destructive checkout operations
   - force push
   - automatic deletion of untracked files
   - overwriting existing user changes.

If the working tree is dirty, inspect and report the modifications before switching branches.

If safe, create a separate working branch:

`feat/phase-3-2-provider-independent-completion`

If branch creation is unsafe because of existing work, stop and report why.

Do not commit, push, create a PR or merge without explicit authorization.

---

# PART 1 — PHASE 3.2A: TRUSTED PRODUCTION BLUEPRINTS

Priority: Highest.

Author complete, independently reviewable production blueprint candidates for the two existing Trace lesson families:

1. `fastapi-payload-mismatch` — Notes.
2. `fastapi-nested-payload-transfer` — Tasks.

## Source-of-truth inspection

Inspect the actual:

- Lesson definitions.
- FastAPI exercise applications.
- Pydantic schemas.
- Database models.
- Verification implementations.
- Evaluators.
- Runtime bindings.
- Existing Phase 2.3 composition logic.
- Existing Phase 2.4 candidate validation logic.

Do not use test fixtures as production definitions.

Use fixtures only to understand schemas and testing conventions.

## Blueprint requirements

Each blueprint must contain the complete fields required by `LessonSpecV1`, including:

- Lesson identity and version.
- Learning objectives.
- Scenario and educational content.
- Code artifacts and references.
- Checkpoint topology.
- Prediction choices.
- Misconception definitions.
- Progressive hints.
- Trusted explanations.
- Completion policy.
- Tutor policy.
- Private runtime bindings.
- Evaluation configuration.
- Verification criteria.
- Transfer relationships.
- Pinned references and canonical digests.

Only use facts established by the actual implementation or approved requirements.

Do not invent runtime expectations, database values or evaluator rules.

## Validation

Use the existing Phase 2.3 and 2.4 infrastructure.

Verify:

- Strict schema validation.
- Canonical SHA-256 integrity.
- Identity and version consistency.
- Complete component references.
- Trusted configuration preservation.
- Runtime binding integrity.
- Transfer references.
- Notes runtime execution.
- Tasks runtime execution.
- Independent database readback.
- All applicable evidence categories.

Candidates must remain unpublished and unassigned.

`release_eligible` must remain false.

Do not introduce a production registry or learner endpoint in this milestone.

---

# PART 2 — PHASE 3.2B: GENERATED CANDIDATE LIFECYCLE AND PROVENANCE

Priority: High.

Prepare the backend lifecycle needed to handle future AI-generated lesson candidates.

Inspect the existing generation contracts, composition service and validation service first.

Implement the smallest compatible extension needed to preserve:

- Candidate identity.
- Source blueprint identity and version.
- Source blueprint digest.
- Composition input provenance.
- Generation request identity.
- Candidate creation timestamp.
- Validation result.
- Candidate state.
- Review status.
- Release eligibility.
- Rejection reasons.
- Auditability.

## Lifecycle requirements

Maintain strict separation between:

1. Generated content.
2. Composed candidate.
3. Validated candidate.
4. Human-reviewed candidate.
5. Production-approved candidate.
6. Learner-assigned exercise.

Successful technical validation must NOT automatically mean production approval.

Generated candidate content must never modify trusted runtime or evaluator configuration.

Only permit state transitions that are explicitly defined and tested.

## Persistence

Inspect whether existing repository abstractions and database models support this.

Introduce a migration only if genuinely necessary.

Requirements:

- Preserve existing learner records.
- Never modify historical learner sessions.
- Keep existing schemas backward compatible.
- Use database transactions correctly.
- Test migration upgrades and downgrades.
- Test duplicate and idempotent operations.
- Test invalid state transitions.
- Do not run migrations against the user's real database during development tests.

Do not persist or promote test-only lesson fixtures as production content.

No learner assignment or publication in this milestone.

---

# PART 3 — PHASE 3.3A: TUTORING TRANSACTION SAFETY

Priority: High.

Address the issue discovered in the Phase 3.1B architecture audit.

The current learner hint flow can hold a SQLite write transaction while waiting for a tutor provider call.

This is unsafe for future network-based AI operations.

Refactor the flow so that slow external work does not hold the learner database write lock.

## Required design

Inspect the existing learning service, repository transaction abstractions and tutor interfaces before modifying anything.

Design a safe reservation/finalization flow:

1. Authenticate and authorize the learner session.
2. Validate lesson and hint policy.
3. Reserve an idempotent tutoring operation.
4. Release the learner write transaction.
5. Invoke the tutor abstraction outside the database write transaction.
6. Validate the tutor result.
7. Finalize the interaction in a short transaction.
8. Return a backward-compatible response.

Handle:

- Duplicate requests.
- Concurrent requests.
- Provider timeouts.
- Provider exceptions.
- Invalid tutor responses.
- Retried requests.
- Partial failures.
- Learner session expiration.
- Hint-policy enforcement.

Do not weaken ownership checks.

Do not allow an LLM to bypass authored hint policies.

Preserve existing learner API behaviour wherever possible.

Continue using the deterministic fake tutor in tests.

Add concurrency and failure-injection tests.

---

# PART 4 — PHASE 3.3B: APPLICATION-SCOPED AI LIFECYCLE

Prepare the shared AI infrastructure for future provider integration.

Use the existing Phase 3.1A contracts.

Inspect the application startup/shutdown lifecycle and dependency injection architecture.

Implement only what is necessary to establish:

- Application-scoped AI client ownership.
- Shared client reuse.
- Explicit mock and disabled operation modes.
- Correct startup and shutdown behaviour.
- Dependency injection into internal services.
- Configuration validation.
- Deterministic test isolation.
- Clean resource disposal.

The client must not be recreated independently for every request.

Do not silently activate live provider mode.

Do not install provider SDKs.

Do not introduce credentials.

Do not make network requests.

Do not add new public AI endpoints merely to prove that dependency injection works.

All default production behaviour must remain safe when live AI is not configured.

---

# PART 5 — PHASE 3.4A: SUPPORT KNOWLEDGE BASE READINESS

Prepare the support assistant for eventual use without activating an unapproved production knowledge base.

Inspect the existing SupportAssistant and retrieval contracts.

Create a structured draft documentation package covering:

1. Trace overview and product capabilities.
2. Account registration and authentication.
3. Google authentication troubleshooting.
4. Learning session navigation.
5. Prediction, attempts and verification.
6. Hints and lesson explanations.
7. Progress and retrieval exercises.
8. Daily exercise availability.
9. Common technical problems.
10. Human support and escalation procedure.

Do not invent operational policies or support contact details.

Mark unapproved documents as draft.

Create deterministic retrieval evaluation fixtures for:

- Exact source matching.
- Relevant source selection.
- Unsupported questions.
- Conflicting documents.
- Missing documents.
- Stale documentation.
- Source reference integrity.
- Prompt injection within retrieved content.

Ensure retrieved documents remain untrusted data.

The LLM must never be allowed to choose arbitrary files or bypass source allowlists.

Do not expose document selection to clients.

Do not activate production support while the approved corpus and escalation destination are missing.

---

# PART 6 — DAILY EXERCISE POLICY READINESS

Prepare the backend for the product rule:

A learner may receive at most THREE new exercise assignments per learner-local calendar day.

Important distinctions:

- New assignments consume the quota.
- Hints do not consume the quota.
- Explanations do not consume the quota.
- Retries do not consume the quota.
- Existing exercises do not consume the quota.
- Retrieval and review exercises do not consume the quota unless explicitly classified as new assignments.

Inspect the current identity, timezone, lesson assignment and scheduling architecture.

Implement only where the authoritative learner-local timezone and assignment semantics are established.

Requirements:

- Server-side enforcement.
- Concurrency safety.
- Idempotent assignment creation.
- Correct local-day boundaries.
- UTC-safe persistence.
- Tests across midnight and timezone boundaries.
- No reliance on frontend enforcement.

Do not use the server's local timezone as a substitute for the learner's timezone.

If a required timezone policy, account field or assignment model is missing, prepare the service contract and document the unresolved decision rather than inventing a policy.

Do not create assignments from unapproved or unvalidated candidates.

Do not assign test fixtures in production.

---

# PART 7 — PERSONALIZATION READINESS

Prepare the deterministic backend inputs for future personalized generation.

Do not implement live LLM personalization.

Inspect existing learner profiles and evidence collection.

Identify which trusted signals can support:

- Learner interests.
- Current skill level.
- Previous misconceptions.
- Attempt history.
- Recent success and failure.
- Preferred exercise context.
- Difficulty progression.

Establish strict boundaries:

- Interests may change scenario framing.
- Interests must not change the learning objective.
- Interests must not change evaluator correctness.
- Learner history must be bounded.
- Do not expose private evaluation configuration.
- Do not pass unnecessary personal data into future prompts.
- Do not invent an interest taxonomy without an approved product vocabulary.

Where requirements are missing, prepare a technical proposal rather than introducing arbitrary fields or migrations.

---

# PART 8 — PRESERVE EXISTING RETRIEVAL BEHAVIOUR

Do not change the existing retrieval schedule in this task.

# TRACE — PHASE 3.5B: EVIDENCE-GROUNDED AI FEEDBACK

## Mission

Implement the provider-independent foundation for evidence-grounded feedback in Trace.

Use the completed Phase 3.5A Engineering Evidence V1 implementation as the authoritative source of learner evidence.

Trace must be able to explain:

- What the learner actually demonstrated.
- What they got wrong.
- Why the observed result occurred.
- What remains unknown or unproven.
- What the learner should investigate or attempt next.

The central rule:

**AI explains trusted evidence. AI does not create, modify or overrule evidence.**

Use deterministic mock providers. No live LLM provider, credentials, SDK installation or paid API calls.

This milestone must prepare the internal feedback architecture for eventual provider integration without activating learner-facing AI grading.

---

# PART 0 — REPOSITORY SAFETY AND BASELINE

The completed Phase 3.1A–3.4 implementation is merged into main.

Phase 3.5A is complete but remains uncommitted on:

`feat/phase-3-5a-engineering-evidence`

Its implementation is based on verified merged main:

`897bd02fc23006a9254bccb997f4134eebadf29b`

The existing Phase 3.5A work must be preserved.

Before implementation:

1. Inspect Git branch, HEAD and working tree.
2. Identify every Phase 3.5A uncommitted file.
3. Read:
   - `docs/architecture/engineering-evidence-v1.md`
   - `docs/architecture/engineering-evidence-sources.md`
4. Read the provider-independent completion report.
5. Verify all six competency definitions.
6. Verify evidence extraction and provenance rules.
7. Run the Phase 3.5A focused tests.
8. Confirm the 2,303-test regression baseline if the checkout permits.

Do not:
- Reset or clean the working tree.
- Drop or overwrite existing Phase 3.5A changes.
- Rebase or switch away from the current branch.
- Rewrite existing migrations.
- Delete shared logs.
- Stage, commit, push, create a PR or merge.

Continue on the current branch if safe.

If the repository contains conflicting work, stop and report it.

---

# PART 1 — ESTABLISH THE FEEDBACK ARCHITECTURE

Inspect the existing:

- EngineeringEvidenceService.
- EngineeringEvidenceBatch and source contracts.
- Competency taxonomy.
- LessonAssistant.
- TutorProvider.
- QuestionGenerator.
- Shared AI client and application resources.
- SupportAssistant.
- Lesson definitions and private evaluator boundaries.

Do not create a parallel learner-evidence system.

Do not duplicate raw learner history unnecessarily.

Design a dedicated internal `EvidenceFeedbackService` and provider-neutral feedback contract if the existing interfaces cannot safely support this capability.

Keep these responsibilities separate:

1. Evidence extraction.
2. Deterministic evidence interpretation.
3. Feedback context construction.
4. Optional provider-generated explanatory language.
5. Output validation.
6. Safe presentation-ready response.

The backend must determine which claims are supported before any AI call.

---

# PART 2 — FEEDBACK CONTRACT

Define a frozen, strict, versioned feedback request and response schema.

The request should include, where available:

- Stable request ID.
- Authenticated learner and session identity.
- Pinned lesson identity and version.
- Exact lesson digest.
- Relevant attempt or checkpoint reference.
- Relevant competency.
- Validated evidence references.
- Deterministically established correctness.
- Verification facts.
- Assistance context.
- Bounded trusted lesson content.
- Feedback policy version.

Do not accept evidence, competency labels, correctness or runtime results directly from an untrusted client.

The service must build the request from the existing authenticated principal and EngineeringEvidenceService.

The response should represent five possible sections:

### A. What you demonstrated

Specific learner actions supported by evidence.

### B. What needs improvement

A specific discrepancy, misconception or incomplete action established by trusted observations.

### C. Why it happened

An explanation using actual execution results and approved lesson material.

### D. What to try next

One actionable investigation or learning step.

### E. Evidence references

The precise evidence IDs and trusted lesson references supporting the feedback.

Sections may be absent when evidence is unavailable.

Include explicit feedback states:

- `supported`
- `partially_supported`
- `insufficient_evidence`
- `unavailable`

Do not treat an absent observation as a failed competency.

---

# PART 3 — DETERMINISTIC EVIDENCE INTERPRETATION

Create a deterministic policy layer that controls all substantive feedback claims.

Examples:

| Observed evidence | Allowed interpretation |
|---|---|
| Incorrect prediction and known actual result | Explain the discrepancy |
| Correct prediction, no code change | Confirm prediction only |
| Modification submitted, no successful execution | Modification occurred; implementation success remains unproven |
| Failed execution followed by a passing changed attempt | Report observed recovery, not proven diagnosis |
| Verification passed | State the exact passed checks |
| Hint requested but not delivered | Do not claim hint assistance |
| Tutor assistance delivered before action | Attribute assistance accurately |
| Investigation not captured | Investigation ability unknown |
| Transfer task completed successfully | Report the actual transfer result |
| No supporting evidence | Abstain from the claim |

Respect the Phase 3.5A limitations:

- No captured investigation actions.
- No actual submitted code patch.
- Debugging recovery is chronology only.
- No independent-work proof.
- No exact parent Notes-session linkage.
- Authored-help and external-help visibility are incomplete.
- Unknown assistance remains unknown.

Never turn these limitations into positive or negative competency judgments.

Do not create artificial evidence to make feedback sound more comprehensive.

---

# PART 4 — CONTROLLED AI PROVIDER INTERFACE

Use the existing Phase 3.1A shared infrastructure and Phase 3.3B application-scoped resources.

Create a dedicated provider protocol if necessary, such as `EvidenceFeedbackProvider`.

Do not break existing TutorProvider or LessonAssistant contracts.

The feedback pipeline must work without a live model.

Recommended flow:

1. Authenticate learner.
2. Validate learner/session ownership.
3. Extract trusted evidence using EngineeringEvidenceService.
4. Determine supported claims through deterministic policy.
5. Build a bounded context using pinned trusted lesson content.
6. Invoke a deterministic mock provider.
7. Validate returned structured content.
8. Verify every cited evidence and lesson reference.
9. Return a safe result.

The model may help phrase the explanation.

The model may not:
- Determine correctness.
- Create verification results.
- Invent learner actions.
- Create evidence IDs.
- Change the lesson version.
- Assign competency scores.
- Override deterministic policy.
- Reveal private evaluation configuration.

---

# PART 5 — OUTPUT VALIDATION AND SECURITY

Implement strict structured-output validation.

Validate:

- Exact schema version.
- Allowed feedback state.
- Bounded text length.
- Valid section structure.
- Evidence references exist in the request bundle.
- Lesson references match the pinned lesson.
- No unsupported observations are presented as facts.
- No private evaluator details are disclosed.
- No arbitrary links or document paths are introduced.

Invalid evidence references must cause rejection or safe fallback.

Do not silently discard invalid citations while retaining their conclusions.

Treat learner submissions, code, explanations and retrieved documents as untrusted data.

Add tests for prompt injection and attempts to manipulate the feedback provider.

All source evidence must originate from trusted backend repositories.

---

# PART 6 — NOTES AND TASKS INTEGRATION

Build deterministic feedback scenarios using actual lesson implementations.

## Notes: fastapi-payload-mismatch

Cover:

- Incorrect prediction.
- Pydantic validation failure.
- Incorrect payload modification.
- Successful correction.
- HTTP response evidence.
- Independent database readback.
- Claims that must remain unsupported.

## Tasks: fastapi-nested-payload-transfer

Cover:

- Flat versus nested payload misunderstanding.
- Incorrect prediction.
- Failed execution.
- Modified mapping.
- Successful execution.
- Actual verification evidence.
- Transfer result.
- Missing parent-session relationship.

Never invent lesson facts or runtime results.

Use pinned legacy lesson definitions and exact digests.

Unassigned blueprint candidates must not be substituted for historical lesson versions.

---

# PART 7 — DETERMINISTIC MOCK AND EVALUATION SUITE

Implement a deterministic feedback provider with controllable outputs.

Cover:

- Fully supported feedback.
- Partially supported feedback.
- Insufficient evidence.
- Unavailable feedback.
- Invalid evidence ID.
- Invalid lesson reference.
- Conflicting conclusions.
- Unsupported competence claim.
- Malformed output.
- Oversized output.
- Provider timeout.
- Provider exception.
- Prompt injection.
- Attempts to expose private evaluator rules.

Build an evaluation suite that checks factual grounding rather than generic fluency.

Metrics should include:

- Evidence-reference validity.
- Unsupported-claim rejection.
- Assistance attribution accuracy.
- Abstention accuracy.
- Lesson-version consistency.
- Verification-claim consistency.
- Privacy leakage rejection.

Do not introduce an arbitrary model-quality score.

---

# PART 8 — APPLICATION AND API BOUNDARY

Use application-scoped resources.

Do not create a new public AI endpoint just to demonstrate the service.

Keep feedback internal unless an existing authenticated learner API can safely expose the required capability without breaking contracts.

If a future public endpoint would require a new contract, document it for a separate integration milestone.

Do not introduce persistence unless an explicit architectural need exists.

If persistence is justified:
- Use additive migrations.
- Preserve historical records.
- Test idempotency.
- Test transaction boundaries.
- Use disposable databases only.

Do not alter existing learner hint behaviour.

---

# PART 9 — STRICTLY OUT OF SCOPE

Do not:

- Integrate a live LLM provider.
- Install a provider SDK.
- Add credentials.
- Make paid API requests.
- Generate learner competency scores.
- Implement mastery thresholds.
- Implement adaptive difficulty.
- Implement Phase 3.5C.
- Implement Phase 3.5D.
- Build new frontend screens.
- Add interest-based personalization.
- Change daily assignment policy.
- Change the +1/+7 retrieval schedule.
- Change lesson runtimes.
- Publish or assign unapproved candidates.

---

# PART 10 — TESTING AND QUALITY GATES

Run focused tests first.

Then run the full backend suite and compare against the verified Phase 3.5A baseline:

- 2,303 backend tests.
- 97.35% coverage.
- Ruff/formatting across 187 Python files.
- Mypy across 127 source files.
- OpenAPI: 21 paths and 23 operations.

The exact final baseline must be freshly reproduced before declaring regression results.

Also run:
- Existing migration tests.
- Ownership and security tests.
- Tutor transaction/concurrency tests.
- Notes and Tasks runtime tests.
- Evidence integrity tests.
- Mock-provider evaluation suite.
- API/OpenAPI comparison.

Do not lower coverage thresholds or weaken existing assertions.

If the baseline changes, explain precisely why.

---

# PART 11 — FINAL REPORT

Report:

1. Phase 3.5A prerequisite verification.
2. New feedback contracts.
3. Deterministic policy rules.
4. Evidence sources consumed.
5. Notes feedback coverage.
6. Tasks feedback coverage.
7. Mock provider implementation.
8. Output validation and security guarantees.
9. Files created and modified.
10. Database changes, if any.
11. Tests added and results.
12. Full regression results.
13. Coverage and static checks.
14. OpenAPI comparison.
15. Remaining instrumentation gaps.
16. Git branch and working-tree status.

Distinguish:
- Deterministically established facts.
- Mock-generated explanatory text.
- Provider integration not yet implemented.
- Unsupported claims that correctly produce abstention.

Do not commit, push or merge.

## DEFINITION OF DONE

Trace can transform validated engineering evidence into bounded, structured, evidence-grounded feedback through an internal provider-neutral service.

The deterministic backend establishes what is true; the provider only helps explain it.

Missing evidence produces explicit uncertainty rather than fabricated learner assessment.

No live AI provider, arbitrary competency score or public learner-facing AI grading is activated.

The failure output shows the substring `422` inside a UUID:

Replace the broad substring assertion with a structural check.

The test should verify that:

1. Raw HTTP status values are not present as actual data values.
2. Raw validation error strings are absent.
3. Raw observed response payloads are absent.
4. Private evaluator or request data is not exposed.
5. Legitimate UUIDs and source references remain valid.

Do not merely change `"422"` to another substring.

Do not remove the security assertion.

# TRACE — Generate Frontend API Integration Handoff for Sohaib

I need a complete, accurate API integration document for our frontend developer.

Sohaib is responsible for building the functional frontend and integrating the existing backend APIs. He needs to know exactly what is implemented and ready to consume.

## Task

Inspect the actual FastAPI backend implementation, API routers, dependencies, Pydantic schemas, authentication logic, configuration and relevant tests.

Use the existing OpenAPI specification as a cross-check. The current backend has 21 paths and 23 operations.

Create:

1. `docs/frontend-api-integration.md`
2. A machine-readable OpenAPI JSON export at an appropriate documentation path, generated from the actual running application schema if possible.

## The documentation must contain

### A. API inventory

For every actual HTTP endpoint, document:

- HTTP method and exact path.
- Purpose and functionality.
- Implementation status.
- Authentication requirements.
- Required permissions and ownership rules.
- Request parameters and body schemas.
- Response schema with realistic examples.
- Possible HTTP errors and their meanings.

### B. Authentication

Explain the actual frontend authentication flow:

- Supabase JWT requirements, if applicable.
- Authorization header format.
- Guest-session behavior and history claiming, if implemented.
- Token expiry and unauthorized responses.
- Relevant frontend configuration variable NAMES, never secret values.

### C. Learning APIs

Give special attention to the existing Notes and Tasks learning runtimes:

- Lesson discovery and lesson details.
- Session creation and lifecycle.
- Prediction submission.
- Attempts and code changes.
- Execution and verification.
- Hints.
- Progress.
- Retrieval/revisit functionality.

Only document features and endpoints that actually exist. Do not invent paths or payloads.

### D. Frontend integration guide

For each available endpoint, explain:

- Which frontend screen or user action should call it.
- When to call it.
- Whether it is session-specific.
- How to handle loading, success, empty and error states.
- Whether retries are safe.
- Any sequencing requirements between calls.

Include concise TypeScript request/response examples derived from the real schemas.

### E. Explicit availability classification

Classify every feature as:

- READY: implemented, exposed through HTTP and usable by the frontend.
- PARTIAL: exposed but with limitations or unfinished dependencies.
- INTERNAL ONLY: backend service exists but no public HTTP endpoint exists.
- NOT IMPLEMENTED: no working implementation exists.

Pay particular attention to the new Phase 3.5C.1 and 3.5C.2 work.

The internal evidence-feedback and public-projection foundations must NOT be described as frontend APIs. There is no public learner feedback endpoint yet.

Do not imply that a live LLM provider, production AI functionality, support corpus or daily assignment quota is active unless the actual code proves it.

### F. Developer setup

Document:

- How to start the backend.
- Local API base URL, based on actual project configuration.
- How to access OpenAPI/Swagger documentation.
- CORS requirements.
- Required environment variable NAMES.
- How the frontend should configure its API client.

Never reveal environment variable values, credentials, tokens, database secrets or API keys.

### G. Integration priorities

Recommend the order Sohaib should integrate the actual available APIs, starting with the Notes and Tasks learner experience.

Identify blockers and APIs that need coordination with me.

## Accuracy and safety requirements

- Inspect source code, not just route names.
- Cross-check schemas against actual implementation and tests.
- Never invent endpoints, response fields or authentication behavior.
- Clearly separate public HTTP APIs from internal services.
- Do not modify application implementation.
- Do not change migrations, environment files, databases or existing reports.
- Preserve my current six-file uncommitted Phase 3.5C changes.
- Do not stage, commit, push, merge, reset or clean anything.
- Do not rerun the complete backend test suite.

Finally, provide a summary of the exact files created, the API inventory count, and any details that could not be verified.

### Remaining Phase 3.5C work

C1 and C2 are complete and merged in PR #12. Treat them as completed foundations.

Finish the remaining stages:

**C3 — Operational security**

- Rate limits
- Concurrency limits
- Request/provider deadlines
- Sanitized errors
- `Cache-Control: no-store`
- Correct handling of provider failures and timeouts

**C4 — Authenticated feedback API**

- Implement `POST /api/v1/sessions/{session_id}/feedback`.

- Enforce ownership and authentication.

**C5 — Frontend integration**

- No invented endpoints or misleading mock responses presented as live features.

**C6 — End-to-end security and release validation**

- Test authentication and authorization.
- Test session ownership and answer visibility.
- Test privacy and citation filtering.
- Test rate limits, concurrency and timeout behaviour.
- Test provider failures and safe fallbacks.
- Run the focused tests and relevant regression suite.

### Execution constraints

1. Inspect the current branch, working tree and existing implementation before editing.
2. Preserve unrelated local changes and untracked work.
3. Do not use destructive Git commands.
4. Do not create duplicate APIs or duplicate authorization logic.

**Deadline: 4:00 PM PKT. Report blockers immediately rather than silently reducing security requirements.**

**Keep the key on the backend. Adding it alone won’t activate AI yet—Trace currently supports only disabled/mock providers.**

For now, store it safely in Windows:

1. Search **“Edit environment variables for your account”**.
2. Under **User variables**, click **New**:
   - Name: `GEMINI_API_KEY`
   - Value: paste your key privately.
3. Open a fresh PowerShell window afterward.

Google documents this environment-variable approach. [Official instructions](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/api-key)

**Don’t put it in `.env.local`, any `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` variable, chat or Git.** Also, don’t add it to `backend/.env` yet: Trace’s strict settings loader doesn’t currently accept that setting.

The next engineering step is implementing the Gemini backend adapter and connecting the required APIs. Before live calls, we still need the **exact Gemini model(s)** and your **approved spending limit**. The key stays server-side throughout.

## My request:
TRACE — Finish Live Gemini Integration and Support RAG

Continue the existing implementation. Do not restart it or
replace the architecture.

For this three-hour work block, focus on LLM integration,
RAG and the API connections needed to make them work.

CURRENT CHECKPOINT

- Working branch: codex/gemini-free-tier-integration.
- 15 backend files changed/added locally; preserve them.
- 106 focused transport/client tests passed.
- One mypy error and lint cleanup remain.
- Tutor route integration/security tests remain.
- Frontend wiring and API contract updates remain.
- Full regression and a real Gemini test remain.
- PR #15 and PR #16 are merged; do not redo them.
- API handoff commit 75b41d1 is pushed on
  codex/frontend-integration-readiness. Preserve that separate
  work and inspect it read-only where useful.

APPROVED PROVIDER CONFIGURATION

- Gemini model: gemini-3.5-flash-lite.
- Credential variable: GEMINI_API_KEY.
- Free-tier project with no billing.
- No paid usage, billing activation or paid fallback.

These choices are already approved. Do not ask for them again
unless you discover a concrete incompatibility.

Never print the key, include it in logs, commit it, or expose
it through frontend code or API responses.

1. PRESERVE AND UNBLOCK

Inspect the branch, HEAD and full working-tree state.
Preserve all existing implementation, logs and unrelated work.

Check credential availability without displaying its value.
If missing, give precise private configuration instructions
and continue independent implementation work.

I authorize applying the previously tested existing migrations
0005–0007 to my local development database, upgrading it from
0004 to 0007. Confirm the backup exists first, preserve existing
records, and verify database integrity and backend readiness.
Do not reset, recreate or delete the database.

Inspect current origin/main safely. Do not blindly pull,
switch branches or overwrite the unfinished Gemini changes.

2. FINISH THE GEMINI ADAPTER

Resolve the current mypy error and lint issues.
Complete the real provider connection through the existing
shared AI client and application lifecycle.

Preserve request/token bounds, timeouts, usage controls,
safe errors and existing deterministic exercise verification.

The enabled AI application paths must call Gemini.
Provider failures must return honest unavailable/error states.

3. COMPLETE THE LESSON TUTOR FLOW

Finish the protected tutor endpoint and minimal frontend
adapter wiring required for the existing tutor panel.

Ground requests in the owned session, pinned lesson and
permitted learner context.

Test authentication, ownership, answer visibility, invalid
requests, rate limits, provider failures and timeouts.
Keep Gemini separate from exercise correctness and completion.

Preserve Sohaib’s existing design.

4. COMPLETE SUPPORT RAG

Reuse the existing support retrieval architecture.

Use approved customer-facing Trace documentation.
Exclude agent logs, environment files, secrets and private
implementation material.

Return source references. Unsupported questions should receive
an honest insufficient-information response.

If source approval is genuinely required, prepare the exact
document list and proposed corpus for review while continuing
the other implementation work.

Expose and document the protected support API and connect
the existing support adapter where feasible.

5. GENERATION CONNECTION

Connect Gemini to the existing question/lesson-generation
interface and trusted validation pipeline.

Preserve candidate provenance, validation and publication rules.
Clearly report any missing generation delivery or persistence
work. Do not silently mark the complete feature finished.

6. VALIDATE AND HAND OFF

Run affected focused tests after fixes, then the relevant full
regression and frontend checks.

Perform minimal, bounded real Gemini checks using synthetic
or public content under the approved free-tier configuration.

Verify actual API flows and frontend responses.
Update OpenAPI, generated API types and integration examples.

Provide safe environment templates with placeholders.
Document how real credentials are configured privately.

FINAL REPORT

Return:
- Implemented and live-tested capabilities.
- Actual API paths, authentication and request/response examples.
- RAG sources and retrieval evidence.
- Test, lint, type-check and regression results.
- Database upgrade/readiness results.
- Exact remaining blockers and unfinished features.
- Branch and changed-file summary.
- Configuration and handoff instructions for Sohaib.

Keep leaderboard, GitHub integration, UI redesign, competency
scoring and new daily-assignment systems outside this block.

Do not weaken checks to meet the timebox.
Do not commit, push, merge or deploy yet.
Finish with a review-ready implementation and evidence.

Gemini tutoring and support RAG are integrated, connected to the existing UI, and **live-tested**.

- `POST /api/v1/sessions/{session_id}/tutor/ask`
- `POST /api/v1/support/ask`

Both require account Bearer authentication and accept `{"question":"..."}`. Support returns citations from the approved **9 articles / 19 passages**. Your key is loaded privately on the backend.

Validation completed:

- **3,387 backend tests passed**, 96.93% coverage—0.41 points below the recorded baseline; threshold unchanged.
- **140 frontend tests passed**.
- **58 existing browser tests passed**, four existing skips; **four new AI walkthroughs passed**.
- Ruff, formatting, mypy, ESLint, type checks and production build passed.
- Database upgraded **0004 → 0007**, with backup, records preserved and integrity checks passing.
- OpenAPI: **24 paths / 26 operations**, preserving previous contracts.

Generation successfully produced a validated candidate, but publication, assignment and learner delivery remain unfinished. The tutor provides bounded approved guidance.

[Sohaib’s API/configuration guide](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/docs/frontend-gemini-integration.md) · [Full report and file inventory](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/docs/architecture/gemini-free-tier-integration-report.md)

The initial Phase 3.8 audit is complete. **The working AI implementation remains unchanged.**

Created `codex/phase-3-8-llm-rag-infrastructure` from verified `origin/main` at `b117773`—the PR #17 merge. Existing logs, environments, credentials and databases were preserved.

The main findings:

| Area | Verified gap |
|---|---|
| Provider deadlines | HTTP timeouts work, but a slowly progressing provider response can outlive its total budget. Shutdown does not drain admitted workers before disposing resources. |
| Usage accounting | Refusal/truncated responses discard supplied token and model metadata. Confirmed with an offline SDK reproduction. |
| Retrieval | All **8 existing keyword anchors** rank the expected document first, but exploratory paraphrases miss relevant passages. These tests cannot establish passage recall or answer quality. |
| Knowledge governance | Citations lack immutable document-version/digest references. The full review/publication/removal lifecycle remains unimplemented. |
| Tutor | Context is limited; stage-specific progressive guidance is not server-enforced. The legacy hint API remains explicitly fixture-based. |
| Deployment | Only development/test configuration and SQLite are supported. PostgreSQL/pgvector would require a separate architecture decision. |

**349 focused tests passed.** No live provider calls were made; the full regression/browser suites were not rerun.

The proposed order is:

1. Fix usage accounting and safe error classification.
2. Harden absolute provider deadlines, shared admission, shutdown and diagnostics.
3. Review passage-level gold labels and freeze the lexical benchmark before changing ranking.
4. Add versioned knowledge provenance and governed publication.
5. Improve measured lexical retrieval and server-controlled tutor context.
6. Consider semantic retrieval only after benchmark evidence and explicit embedding/cost approval.

**No embedding provider or vector database is selected.**

Review materials:

- [Audit, provider findings and implementation plan](<CODEX_HOME>/visualizations/2026/10/30/01a18c8a-4fc7-70a0-bf35-3113cfa88f29/phase38-initial-audit-c8hn1tns/initial-audit-and-plan.txt)
- [Benchmark design](<CODEX_HOME>/visualizations/2026/10/30/01a18c8a-4fc7-70a0-bf35-3113cfa88f29/phase38-initial-audit-c8hn1tns/benchmark-design.txt) · [Measured baseline](<CODEX_HOME>/visualizations/2026/10/30/01a18c8a-4fc7-70a0-bf35-3113cfa88f29/phase38-initial-audit-c8hn1tns/lexical-baseline.json)
- [Retrieval ADR, knowledge lifecycle and deployment proposal](<CODEX_HOME>/visualizations/2026/10/30/01a18c8a-4fc7-70a0-bf35-3113cfa88f29/phase38-initial-audit-c8hn1tns/knowledge-retrieval-deployment-proposal.txt)
- [Sohaib’s frontend coordination handoff](<CODEX_HOME>/visualizations/2026/10/30/01a18c8a-4fc7-70a0-bf35-3113cfa88f29/phase38-initial-audit-c8hn1tns/frontend-coordination.txt)

You need **two main services for local Trace**, plus Google OAuth if you want Google sign-in.

| Configuration | What it does | Where it belongs |
|---|---|---|
| **`GEMINI_API_KEY`** | Powers Gemini tutoring, support answers and generation. The embedding adapter uses the same credential. | `backend/.env.gemini` — backend only |
| **`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`** and **`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`** | Connect the frontend to your shared Supabase project for registration and login. | Root `.env.local` |
| **`TRACE_SUPABASE_URL`**, **`TRACE_SUPABASE_ISSUER`**, **`TRACE_SUPABASE_JWKS_URL`**, **`TRACE_SUPABASE_AUDIENCE`** | Let the backend verify signed-in users. These are configuration settings, not additional secret keys. | `backend/.env` |
| **Google OAuth Client ID + Client Secret** | Enable **Continue with Google**. These are separate from your Gemini key. | Supabase’s Google authentication provider settings |

Get Gemini credentials through [Google AI Studio](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/api-key), Supabase configuration from your [project’s API settings](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/getting-started/api-keys), and follow [Supabase’s Google sign-in setup](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/social-sign-in/auth-google) for OAuth.

**Your machine already has the Gemini key and matching Supabase configuration.** The offline check passed. Google OAuth dashboard configuration still needs separate verification. Semantic retrieval also has quota and validation gates; adding a key alone does not activate it.

For deployment later, you’ll need private PostgreSQL connection credentials and an approved hosting target. Local SQLite needs no database key. No OpenAI, Pinecone or Supabase service-role key is required for the current setup.

Use the exact Gemini location:

`<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/.env.gemini`

Keep secrets out of Git, chat and frontend `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` variables. Sohaib’s instructions are in the [setup guide](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/docs/frontend/sohaib-gemini-setup.md).

**Two blockers are confirmed.** The exact branch that produced your earlier generic screen remains unproven because the callback clears its state and hides all failure categories.

1. **The running backend has GitHub disabled.**
   I inspected the environment of the backend process on port 8000: none of the nine GitHub App variables is loaded. Your Windows User signing secret and encryption key exist and pass format checks, but they have not reached that process.

   [github_settings.py:14](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/core/github_settings.py:14) reads process variables, without an environment file. [main.py:95](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/main.py:95) captures those settings at application creation.

2. **The OAuth exchange contains a code defect.**
   [github_app.py:155](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/ai/providers/github_app.py:155) supplies an empty token, while [line 96](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/ai/providers/github_app.py:96) always adds an Authorization header. This produces `Authorization: Bearer `.

   A no-network check using the actual constructed request and installed HTTP serializer reproduced **`LocalProtocolError`**, which Trace converts to **503 `github_unavailable`**. This would block code exchange after configuration is corrected.

The verified flow is: authenticated initiation → owner-bound, ten-minute state → installation callback → explicit GitHub OAuth → callback validation → single-use state consumption → code exchange → user/installation/repository checks → encrypted grant persistence. See [github_authorization.py](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/backend/app/services/github_authorization.py).

The bare callback address is expected: [github-callback.tsx:15](<TRACE_REPOSITORY>/src/components/projects/github-callback.tsx:15) deliberately removes query parameters before validation.

These variables must reach the **backend launch process**:

```text
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_MODE
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_ID
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_SLUG
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_ID
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_SECRET
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_STATE_SIGNING_SECRET
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY
TRACE_GITHUB_APP_CALLBACK_URL
```

Use `enabled` mode and PEM **contents** for the private-key variable. The existing secrets need not be regenerated.

Both GitHub callback and installation Setup URLs must be:

`http://localhost:3000/projects/github/callback`

Trace’s current two-stage flow requires **“Request user authorization during installation” disabled**. GitHub documents that enabling it replaces the separate Setup URL flow. Your actual dashboard settings remain unverified. [GitHub documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/maintaining-github-apps/modifying-a-github-app-registration)

Other findings:

- Database is healthy at **0013**; no missing migrations.
- Inspected local database contains **zero authorization states, grants or repository connections**.
- Available backend logs contain no failed GitHub completion request.
- **104 existing GitHub tests passed in 36.12 seconds.** Their mock transports miss the malformed-header defect; this is not live authorization verification.

The minimal proposed fix is to omit Authorization **only for OAuth code exchange**, preserve Bearer authentication for GitHub REST calls, and add a serialization regression. Then privately load all settings into the launch terminal, restart the backend, and start a **fresh Connect flow** using the same tab, hostname and Trace account.

No source/configuration/database edits, staging, commits, pushes or deployments were performed. Existing work remains preserved.

We have completed a read-only diagnosis of Trace's GitHub App integration. There are two confirmed blockers.

**BLOCKER 1 — OAuth token exchange defect**

File: `backend/app/ai/providers/github_app.py`

The OAuth code exchange currently supplies an empty token to a request builder that unconditionally adds an Authorization header.

This creates:

`Authorization: Bearer `

The installed HTTP request serializer reproduces `LocalProtocolError`.

**BLOCKER 2 — Backend configuration**

The running backend process does not have any of the nine required GitHub App environment variables loaded. This will be addressed separately through local Windows environment configuration. Do not put credentials in source code or repository configuration files.

### Your implementation task

First inspect the current Git status and branch.

Only proceed when:

- I have confirmed Sohaib has given the go-ahead.
- I have pulled the latest `main` using our agreed workflow.
- There are no unexpected uncommitted changes that could be overwritten.

If these conditions are not satisfied, STOP and report the situation.

Implement the smallest safe code fix:

1. Inspect the OAuth token exchange implementation and its HTTP client/request builder.
2. Ensure the POST request to `https://github.com/login/oauth/access_token` does not contain an Authorization header.
3. Pass the required OAuth exchange parameters correctly: client ID, client secret, authorization code and the existing redirect URI.
4. Preserve the existing Accept and content-type behavior where appropriate.
5. Do not break or remove Bearer authentication from ordinary GitHub REST API calls.
6. Do not globally disable authentication headers or weaken any security checks.
7. Preserve state verification, owner binding, state expiry, single-use consumption, installation validation and encrypted grant persistence.

### Regression tests

Add tests that:

- Construct the actual OAuth exchange request using the installed HTTP library.
- Serialize the request and prove that no LocalProtocolError occurs.
- Assert that the OAuth exchange contains no Authorization header.
- Assert that normal GitHub REST requests still include their expected Bearer authentication.
- Cover unsuccessful token exchange responses without leaking secrets.

Use a local serialization test; do not pretend a mocked token exchange proves live GitHub authorization.

### Restrictions

- Do not change frontend code.
- Do not change database migrations.
- Do not alter the lesson generation or Private Guides implementation.
- Do not change unrelated architecture or provider interfaces.
- Do not request, print, expose or commit credentials.
- Do not modify `.env` files.
- Do not commit, push, merge or deploy.

### Verification and report

Run the focused GitHub tests, then the relevant backend regression suite.

Report:

1. Exact files changed.
2. Root cause and fix.
3. Regression tests added.
4. Exact test results.
5. Any remaining blockers for a live GitHub authorization test.
6. Whether the implementation is ready for real GitHub testing.

Implement the fix, do not merely provide a plan.

**Found the cause: the installation is valid, but Trace lacks a working “authorize an existing installation” flow.**

- **Connect GitHub App always opens** `https://github.com/apps/trace-project-learning/installations/new?state=[redacted]`. It never chooses OAuth directly. [Connect handler](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/src/components/projects/github-projects.tsx:79)
- Trace starts OAuth only after an installation callback supplies an `installation_id`. Without that callback, it cannot advance. [Callback handler](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/src/components/projects/github-callback.tsx:22)
- Your Setup URL and OAuth callback are correct, but **Redirect on update is unchecked**. That explains why saving the existing installation stayed on GitHub. [GitHub’s documented behavior](https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/registering-a-github-app/about-the-setup-url)

Backend evidence confirms **four successful start requests, zero completion requests, zero consumed states and zero saved grants**. The OAuth token exchange has not been reached.

**Next exact action:** keep your existing installation and three selected repositories unchanged. Trace needs a targeted fix that starts fresh user OAuth, discovers the user’s existing installation, validates its ownership and permissions, then saves the encrypted grant. Enabling “Redirect on update” alone won’t provide this missing flow for an unchanged installation.

I have made **no code or settings changes, service restarts, or original-database writes** during this diagnosis.

**Cross-tab state loss is plausible, but the exact trigger is not proven.** The callback never reached the backend, and the old error handler erased the information needed to distinguish missing state from other browser failures.

The implementation works as follows:

- [Start handler](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/src/components/projects/github-projects.tsx:70) saves the returned state, expiry and GitHub URLs in `sessionStorage`, then uses `window.location.assign`—same-tab navigation.
- [Pending-state validation](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/src/lib/api/github-projects.ts:51) requires an exact state match, an unexpired timestamp and an approved authorization URL.
- [Callback](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/src/components/projects/github-callback.tsx:17) clears the address-bar query, validates pending state, then calls the backend. The backend independently validates signed state, ownership, expiry and single-use consumption.

`sessionStorage` is tab-specific; some newly opened tabs initially copy their opener’s storage. Therefore, different tab IDs alone cannot prove state loss. The available history has no opener/initiator information explaining the tab switch. [Browser storage documentation](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/sessionStorage)

**Diagnostics added:** a collapsed “Connection diagnostics” panel now shows only fixed failure labels and presence booleans. It distinguishes missing/malformed state, mismatch, expiry, blocked storage and completion-call failure. No values, URLs, tokens, exception text, console logging or telemetry are included.

**Proposed fix, not implemented:** retain the existing same-tab navigation and ensure the browser handoff stays in that tab. If cross-tab completion must be supported, bind the existing backend one-time state to a short-lived, appropriately scoped HttpOnly flow cookie, retaining all ownership, signature, expiry and replay checks. Do not simply remove the browser check or move state to shared local storage.

Validation: **51 focused tests and all 245 frontend tests passed**, plus ESLint, TypeScript and whitespace checks.

No authorization retry, service restart, original-database access, commit or push occurred. The functional authorization fix remains pending review.

2. **Failure classification:** It distinguishes:

   | Diagnostic stage | Meaning |
   |---|---|
   | `pending_missing` | No pending flow was available |
   | `expired` | Pending flow expired |
   | `state_mismatch` | Returned and saved state differ |
   | `pending_read_failed` | Browser storage could not be read |
   | `pending_json_invalid` | Stored data could not be parsed |
   | `completion_call` | Failure while invoking the completion API |

7. Ensure the UI only reports successful generation after the database transaction commits.

9. Use only the isolated acceptance database. Preserve the original OneDrive database and all backups.

11. Run relevant backend, frontend and regression tests.

Configuration remains **repository-only**. No global installation or settings changes were made.

The complete repository [extract-log.py](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/.claude/hooks/extract-log.py) is:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Compatibility entry point for the shared primary-session-only Claude adapter."""

import runpy
import sys
from pathlib import Path

root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0, str(root / "scripts"))
sys.argv = [str(root / "scripts/agent_session_logs.py"), "hook", "--agent", "claude"]
runpy.run_path(sys.argv[0], run_name="__main__")
```

It delegates to the shared adapter, whose [checkout guard](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/scripts/agent_session_logs.py:283) requires:

```python
cwd = Path(payload.get("cwd", ""))
if not cwd.is_absolute() or project_root(cwd) != ROOT:
    raise ValueError("The hook belongs to another checkout")
```

`project_root()` resolves the actual Git repository root. This rejects unrelated repositories, including a separate Git repository nested inside Trace.

Your four safeguards are covered:

1. **Trace-only logging:** Claude reads only the hook’s exact transcript and validates its session identity and working directory. Output goes only into this checkout’s `.claude-logs/`. There is no background session scan.
2. **Global settings untouched:** The global Claude settings hash is unchanged. Global `extract-log.py` and the project registry remain absent.
3. **Backup before future changes:** The optional [prepared global wrapper and installer](C:/Users/soore/.codex/worktrees/github-live-acceptance/Trace-v1/scripts/setup_agent_session_logging.py:25) defaults to dry-run. If separately approved, it backs up existing files before writing and preserves unrelated settings/hooks. **It has not been applied.**
4. **Shared cohort format:** Both writers use `.claude-logs/<timestamp>_<session-id>.md`, the required frontmatter/statistics and exact `[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=PROMPT ...]` / `RESPONSE` markers. Agent attribution remains `codex` or `claude-code`.

**Verification:** 125 logging tests passed; Ruff, formatting and whitespace checks passed. Genuine Codex prompt/final capture was verified. Claude’s live capture remains unverified because its CLI is signed out.

Nothing was staged, committed or pushed. Global installation will wait for your explicit approval.
